    - the gate end to end: `tsc completed in 761s (exit 2, heap 8192MB): 400 error(s) across 141
      file(s)` then `test typecheck baseline: matched exactly, in both directions`. Because the
      ratchet flags any UNLISTED file carrying an error, that clean result is also the proof that
      the new test file is itself type-clean.
    - production `tsc --noEmit` against `tsconfig.json`: exit 0, no diagnostics.
    - `testTypecheckRatchet.test.ts`: 22 passed, 22 total.
    - mutation, 4 mutants, each applied to a COPY and each restore proved with `cmp`: M1a
      (crashed() stops checking the exit status), M1b (the crash phrase becomes unconditional), M2
      (the diagnostic regex loses its leading-space anchor), M3 (ratchet() stops reporting
      improvements). Each killed EXACTLY ONE named test, with a `Tests: 1 failed` line as the
      second witness. Different tests died for different mutants, so the four rules are
      independent rather than all keying off one assertion.
    - adjacent suites: 297 passed across `services/content/__tests__` plus
      `learnerWriterTripwire` and `writerPairTripwires`, which is where PR #3141 was caught last
      time. `frontendCopyLint` enumerates the directory the new baseline file was added to and
      still passes.
    - the `guards` job locally, each with a non-zero scanned count so a silent no-op cannot read
      as a pass: secret-scan 6,692 files, route-auth 134 route files, explorer copy lint 16
      self-test cases then 77 copy values, citation check 158 citations across 14 of 16 files.
    - artifacts: visual 1 page and document 4 pages, both page counts READ BACK from the rendered
      PDF bytes rather than intended, mermaid confirmed rendered as `<svg>` in both, and both
      looked at as images.
  - Notes: **THE MEASUREMENT ITSELF WAS THE FIRST FALSE PASS.** At the default heap, tsc over this
    program dies after ~490s with a JavaScript heap FATAL ERROR: it prints ZERO diagnostics and
    exits 134. A runner that counts diagnostics reads that as a clean pass. The first run of this
    work reported `TSC_EXIT=134` alongside "0 errors" and was very nearly believed. That is why
    the checker asserts the exit status FIRST and treats anything other than 0 or 2 as a death.
  - Notes: **A FALSE-FAILURE DEFECT I SHIPPED INTO THE CHECKER AND THEN FOUND.** `crashed()`
    originally matched the phrase "FATAL ERROR" (and friends) ANYWHERE in tsc output,
    unconditionally. `grep` then showed those phrases occur in three ordinary `src` files and in
    the checker's own test fixtures, and tsc echoes source text into diagnostics - so a COMPLETED
    run could have been declared a crash, failing CI with the wrong diagnosis and skipping the
    baseline comparison entirely. It failed SAFE, but a gate that cries wolf gets switched off.
    Fixed: the exit status is primary and catches every real heap death on its own; the phrase now
    counts only when no diagnostics parsed at all. Mutant M1b reproduces this exact bug, so the
    test is known to catch it rather than assumed to.
  - Notes: **WHY A BASELINE AND NOT A CLEAN GATE.** 400 pre-existing errors cannot be fixed in
    this PR, and a job that simply failed would be switched off instead of merged, leaving the
    repo where it started with one more disabled check. The table freezes the debt per file and
    gates the only thing still preventable, a new one. It is SHRINK-ONLY: the per-file count must
    EQUAL the frozen number, so a FIX also fails and names the lower number to paste in, and an
    entry that reaches zero must be deleted. A file absent from the table must have zero, so a new
    test file is covered the moment it is written.
  - Notes: **ZERO of the 400 are in `src`.** Production code was already clean under the wider
    program. The entire debt sits in the files that assert other files are correct, which includes
    the guards protecting Person 360 and the Sales Readiness Score.
  - Notes: **CORRECTION, AND IT CHANGES THE DECISION. The cost is 71 SECONDS, not 13 minutes.**
    The first version of this entry, the commit body, the PR and the artifact all said "about 13
    minutes", taken from the local runs (761s and 785s). CI then ran the same typecheck in **71
    seconds**, 121s for the whole job including dependency install. The local machine is Windows,
    with the tree under a syncing folder and two other heavy jobs running against it at the time;
    the runner is a clean Linux box. The figure that bears on whether this belongs in CI is the
    CI one, and at 71s the cost objection largely disappears. Artifact, PR body and ticket comment
    were all corrected, and the two superseded PDFs were trashed on the ticket and replaced rather
    than left sitting beside the corrected pair.
  - Notes: **HOW THAT WAS CAUGHT, because it is the same lesson again.** `gh pr checks` reported
    `Test-file typecheck  pass  2m1s`. A 2-minute green on a check expected to take 13 minutes is
    precisely the shape of a job that skipped its work, so the badge was not believed. Opening the
    job log showed the real line: `tsc completed in 71s (exit 2, heap 8192MB): 400 error(s) across
    141 file(s)` and `matched exactly, in both directions` - the same counts as the local run. A
    job that had skipped the typecheck could not have produced them. The green was real and my
    number was wrong, which is the less common of the two ways this goes.
  - Notes: **FOR ALI, the one judgement call.** It is wired REPORT-ONLY and is NOT a required
    status check, so nothing is blocked by it today. Promoting it is a branch-protection change
    and therefore his call. At 71s the recommendation is to promote it once it has been green on
    main for a week or two, rather than to leave it reporting indefinitely.
  - Notes: **Rollback:** code and config only, no data touched and nothing sent. `git revert`
    removes the job, the config, the baseline and the checker together. Removing only the CI job
    leaves the checker runnable by hand, which is the sensible half-step if the cost is judged not
    worth it.

    discriminator for "who to call next" and is still EXCLUDED by the M24 contract because

    factor at full marks is not "100% ready", so dividing by what was scoreable would invent
    confidence out of thin evidence. The raw total is the score and `coverage` is reported beside

- [x] M27: score freshness, and a correction to my own contract about recency
  - Date: 2026-10-18
  - Session: CC-20261005-r6h2
  - What changed: new `backend/src/services/salesReadiness/srsFreshness.ts` and its 17 tests;
    `srsEngine.ts` gained `evidenceAsOf` on the inputs and `oldestEvidenceAt` on the result;
    `srsInputs.ts` maps it from the Explorer snapshot's `asOfDate`;
    `docs/growth-os-reset/SALES_READINESS_SCORE_CONTRACT.md` Part 6.3 and 7.1 corrected.
  - Verification: re-run AFTER the last edit:
    - `src/services/salesReadiness`: **71 passed, 71 total**; `adminOs` + `salesReadiness`
      together: **417 passed across 25 suites**.
    - **Four mutants, each killing a named test**, restores `cmp`-identical and md5 back to
      baseline: the evidence bound removed (kills "is bound by the EVIDENCE when the evidence is
      older"), the fresh boundary slipped from `<` to `<=`, the negative-age clamp removed, and
      the stale label stripped of its instruction.
    - scoped `tsc -p` with the REAL `tsconfig.test.json` options: exit 0, run before pushing.
    - the M24 binding test still passes (14) after the contract edit, and the citation gate
      reports `citations OK` with no drift.
  - Notes: **RECOMPUTATION CANNOT BUY FRESHNESS, and that is the whole idea.** A score computed a
    minute ago over week-old evidence is not fresh. If freshness were measured from the
    computation alone, re-running the batch would make every score look current without one new
    fact entering the system - the same class of lie as an absent input reading as zero. So the
    effective as-of is the OLDER of the computation and the oldest dated evidence, and the result
    carries `oldestEvidenceAt` so a surface can assess a score from the score alone rather than
    reassembling inputs it no longer has.
  - Notes: **A stale score is labelled, not hidden.** Hiding it leaves a reader with no number and
    no reason, which reads as "nothing to see" rather than "this is old". The label says the age,
    what bound it, and plainly not to rank on it. `unknown` is a fourth state for a score whose
    date does not parse: not fresh, not hidden.
  - Notes: **I CORRECTED MY OWN CONTRACT, and the correction is that it overstated.** Part 6.3 and
    7.1 gave TWO reasons for excluding recency and only one holds. They also blamed the timeline's
    150-row cap. **The cap cannot affect that field:** `getPersonTimeline` assembles with
    `ORDER BY max(occurred_at) DESC` and applies the limit afterwards, so the cap truncates the
    TAIL and `timeline[0]` is always the newest permitted event. The cap genuinely does break a
    COUNT taken from the timeline, which is why `journeyPanel` takes counts from source tables and
    says so in its own docblock - and that is the claim my contract borrowed without checking
    whether a MAX behaves like a count. It does not. The per-caller domain filter is the whole of
    the defect, and it is enough on its own, so recency stays EXCLUDED.
  - Notes: **The M24 and M25 artifact PDFs still carry the overstatement**, because the Basecamp
    token rotated to the CB System bot at 15:00 UTC on 2026-10-17 and the attach script refuses to
    post to Ali's board as anyone else. They need re-rendering and re-attaching once the token is
    Ali's again; the documents in the repo are already correct.
  - Notes: **The freshness thresholds are PROVISIONAL like the weights.** 24h fresh, 72h aging.
    Nothing has measured how fast this evidence actually decays, so they are a stated hypothesis
    and they are versioned with the ruleset rather than hidden in a constant.

  - Date: 2026-10-26

  - What changed: new `backend/src/services/agents/tools/agentEmailTool.ts` (`sendAgentEmail`, `readAgentGate`, `AGENT_EMAIL_TOOL`, `AGENT_DRAFT_STATUS`) and `__tests__/agentEmailTool.test.ts` (26). Ali's directive: the email tool is shared agent infrastructure "that all agents will use to send emails (that has access)", Maya "can draft for now - but eventually she will send on her own".
  - Verification: `tsc --noEmit` clean on the SECOND attempt (see below), read from the output file (`TSC_EXIT=0`, no diagnostics); 26/26 tests; `scripts/checkTestTypecheck.js` reports "baseline: matched exactly, in both directions" with `GATE_EXIT=0`, so the new test file adds zero errors to the frozen 400-across-141.
  - Notes: **IT CANNOT SEND, FOR TWO INDEPENDENT REASONS.** The transport is deliberately unwired - every path returns `drafted`, the fully-cleared one as `transport_not_wired` - and no agent holds the grant (all 266 production rows carry `tools_granted: []`). One test asserts `'sent'` is unreachable anywhere in this change. Landing the decision logic and its tests before the ability to mail strangers is what keeps it reversible.
  - Notes: **THE TOOL MUST FAIL CLOSED BECAUSE EVERY SURROUNDING GOVERNANCE LAYER DOES NOT.** `authorizeAgentAction` is the ABAC chokepoint and its own docblock says it "only BLOCKS when `abac_enforcement === 'enforce'`" and is "SWALLOW-SAFE + FAILS OPEN"; production `abac_enforcement` is **shadow**, so it allows everything and merely records what it would have denied. And `tools_granted` is declared, typed, Ops-editable and version-historied, but **read by no code at all** - this file is its first consumer, which means this file defines what "has access" means.
  - Notes: **THE ONE ASYMMETRY: a suppression block writes NO draft.** Asserted with `expect(query).not.toHaveBeenCalled()`. A draft addressed to someone who unsubscribed is an unsubscribe waiting to be overridden by a tired reviewer. Everything merely UNCERTAIN - no grant, unknown autonomy, agent missing, lookup threw, disabled, paused, no brand scope, unevaluable gate - becomes a draft, because a human can legitimately resolve those.
  - Notes: **BRAND SCOPE IS ONLY DERIVABLE FROM A CAMPAIGN.** `SendRequest` carries no brand or tenant field, so `brandPreferenceGate` reads them off the Campaign; a send with no `campaignId` short-circuits to `no_brand_scope` and skips brand AND consent. That is what `admissionsEmailAgent` does today. So this tool requires a campaign and drafts without one, which surfaces a missing brand campaign as reviewable drafts instead of mail that quietly bypassed consent.
  - Notes: Drafts live in `scheduled_emails` with status `agent_draft` rather than a new table (a new table is ESCALATE-tier). **Verified against `schedulerService` before choosing it**: the sender claims rows with `SET status = 'processing' ... AND status = 'pending'`, so a draft can never be claimed. Asserted both ways in the suite.
  - Notes: **MUTATION TESTING CAUGHT ONE OF MY OWN TESTS BEING USELESS.** Forcing the grant check to always-true failed 3 tests as predicted - but "every production agent holds an empty array" PASSED, because it asserted only `outcome === 'drafted'`, which is true whether or not the grant is enforced while everything drafts. Strengthened it to assert the `reason`, re-mutated, confirmed 4 failures, then restored and proved identical (md5 `9f2a2fe5...`, `cmp` clean).
  - Notes: **JEST GREEN IS NOT EVIDENCE THAT A FILE COMPILES.** The first `tsc` run failed with 7 errors (`Property ... does not exist on type 'never'`) while all 26 tests passed, because ts-jest runs `isolatedModules` and TRANSPILES without typechecking. Cause was mine: `as unknown as typeof agent` is a cast referring to the variable being assigned, which is circular and resolved to `never`. Fixed with a named `AgentGateRow` interface, and the reason is recorded in its docblock so the next reader does not reintroduce it.

Seeing the PR head frozen at `40c34e98` I reported it as a dismissed approval needing a second click, reaching for the PR-head-lag note when a frozen head means MERGED at least as often as it means lagging, and the two are indistinguishable without reading `mergedAt`. So Ali was sent to click something that did not need clicking, while the real problem went unnamed. **The root cause was ordering, not the check:** CLAUDE.md says to regenerate the per-session report as part of the change, and generating it after committing is the only reason a second push existed at all.

A fresh build in a scratch copy matched the published file **exactly** on every field but the timestamp, with a control proving the comparison could detect a difference. Re-checked by CONTENT: the real path returns 171 KB whose first bytes carry `"schema": "colaberry.kb/v1"` and `"count": 217`, while the nonsense path returns a 2.9 KB HTML shell with no schema marker. The hub is public and unauthenticated, so the stale dates and the forbidden wording are visible right now independently of any agent.

So for the sales slice, what a visitor sees can come from `cora_kb_entries` rather than from the export, and that projection drops the entry id entirely, making nothing quoted through it traceable to a row. This change reads the export, which is the surface the hub designates for AI ingestion. Reconciling the two is a separate decision and is not quietly assumed here.

I came close to reporting that the subagent's map had invented its citations - the map was right and my instrument was wrong, and I had run that search with **no positive control**, the exact failure this log already records for URLs. The source-scan guard then surfaced a fourth path that neither the map nor I had: `caseKnowledgeService.findRelevantAdmissionsAnswers`.
  - Notes: **"PUBLIC-FACING BY DEFINITION, SAFE TO TREAT AS DIRECTLY USABLE".** That was the comment justifying `can_auto_answer: true` on every admissions row inside Inbox Intel's case-resolution read. A row quoting the retired offer is public-facing AND must not be repeated, so the one assumption the comment rests on is the one that fails. That function decides which questions can be answered without asking Ali, so an unquotable row arriving as "directly usable" is the worst possible place for the error. Withheld rows are now dropped from the matches entirely rather than returned with a false flag.
  - Notes: **NO FORBIDDEN-WORDING SCREENING, AND THAT IS A DECISION RATHER THAN AN OMISSION.** Three reasons, in order of weight: `explorerQuoteGuard`'s header records the production measurement that of 467 active assets five matched the rule and only ONE was genuine, so a filter is wrong four times out of five; declaring the regulated word in a new runtime file is ratcheted per file by `bannedWordOutboundCopy.test.ts`, which makes a second declaration expensive on purpose; and Maya's own prompt already forbids it. The gate therefore blocks ONLY retired facts, which are specific, agreed, and carry no false positives. Wording screening, if wanted, belongs behind the same rules-as-an-argument shape rather than a third copy of the word.
  - Notes: **THE COVERAGE GUARD FAILED FOR REAL BEFORE IT PASSED, SO NO SYNTHETIC MUTATION WAS NEEDED.** On its first run it named three genuinely ungated readers, one of which was a live quoting path nobody had mapped. Its own control asserts that more than 500 files were scanned and at least four readers found, and names `mayaToolsService.ts` explicitly, because a sweep that matches nothing makes every assertion below it vacuously true - a guard reporting success while guarding nothing.
  - Notes: **ONE EDIT IS CORRECT BUT INERT, AND IS RECORDED AS SUCH.** `buildMayaSystemPrompt`'s knowledge branch now appends unconditionally, but its only runtime caller (`chatService.ts:200`, at conversation start) passes no `userMessage`, and nothing else in `chatService` references one. So that branch does not execute in production today. It is defensive consistency, not a fix, and counting it as a fix would overstate what changed.

- [x] R0 (partial): put the permission gate on the funnel, and the consent ask where it is read
  - Date: 2026-10-29
  - Session: CC-20261005-r6h2
  - What changed: `acquisitionFunnel` gains an `opted_in` stage between "Reached the CRM" and "Came back later", plus a per-campaign `Opted in` column; `CampaignTable` renders it; the frontend `FunnelCampaignRow` mirror gains the field; and the marketing consent checkbox on `PortalFreeSignupPage` moves from the bottom of the form to directly under the email input. Six files. **No consent WORDING changed and the box is still unchecked and still optional** - both of those are Ali's call and are open.
  - Verification: backend jest **17 passed** on `acquisitionFunnel` (was 13, so the four new cases are new coverage); **mutation test** - forcing the gate's count to 0 **failed 2 tests**, reverted by editing forward and re-run green; frontend `react-scripts test` on the signup page **22 passed**; backend `tsc --noEmit` on the stack's own 5.9.3 **TSC_EXIT=0, zero errors** (the harness reported "exit 1", which was `grep -c` counting zero).
  - Notes: **THE LOCAL FRONTEND COMPILER HID A REAL BREAK, AND ONLY A CONTROL CAUGHT IT.** `frontend/node_modules` carries **TypeScript 4.9.5** while CI runs 5.7+. Its run reported **zero `src/` errors** on a change that genuinely was broken: `CampaignTable` read `row.opted_in` while the frontend `FunnelCampaignRow` had no such field, which is a TS2339 that CI would have failed. The clean output meant the compiler never reached the file, not that the file was clean - the same "an empty result and a skipped file look identical" trap this log already records for `tsc --listFiles`. I only found it by checking whether the type actually declared the field. **A frontend typecheck cannot be verified locally in this worktree; CI is the authority for that half.**
  - Notes: **THE TAB-ORDER TEST FIRED, AND ITS OWN COMMENT SAID HOW TO READ THAT.** Moving the checkbox broke `does not disturb the name and email fields it sits beside`, which asserts the input order. Its author had written: a failure of this shape "is the guard working rather than the change being wrong", and recorded that it fired the same way when the phone field was added. What it protects is **name and email staying at indexes 0 and 1**, because `fillAndSubmit` destructures exactly those two - and a checkbox arriving at index 2 leaves that intact. The evidence is that the other **21** tests in the file passed unchanged; had the insertion retargeted the destructuring, many would have failed rather than one. Updated the assertion with the reasoning recorded in place, rather than silently re-baselining it.
  - Notes: **WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO, WHICH IS THE LARGER HALF.** The opt-in rate is **17%** (73 of 423 leads in thirty days: free_signup 347/59, open_house 61/14, apollo 10/0, popup 1/0, catalog_request 1/0, worldoftaxonomy 3/0). Raising it needs either new wording or making the tick required, and the form's own docblock refuses that trade on its own authority: "making it block submission would trade signups for permissions, which is not a trade a bug fix gets to make on its own." Ali chose option B, keep it optional and change wording plus placement; placement shipped here, wording is with him. Three TWC-safe options were put to him.
  - Notes: **CI CAUGHT EXACTLY WHAT I SAID IT WOULD, WHICH IS THE POINT OF SAYING IT.** The first push to #3486 passed nine checks and **failed `Frontend typecheck`**, the one gate I had already recorded as unverifiable in this worktree. The error was `TS2741: Property 'opted_in' is missing` at `performanceMetrics.test.ts:43` - a fixture building a `FunnelCampaignRow` literal without the newly required field. **`Frontend unit tests` PASSED on the same commit**, because CRA's transform is transpile-only and never typechecks, which is the same trap as backend ts-jest. Fixed by defaulting the field in the fixture rather than making it optional on the type: a campaign row with no opt-in figure is the thing worth noticing, and an optional field would let a door earning no permission render as a blank instead of a zero. Grepped every other construction site first; CI's single-error count was accurate.
  - Notes: **WHY THE GATE IS A STAGE AND NOT A TILE.** The page showed 313 reaching the CRM and 6 coming back, with no reason on screen for the collapse. Most of that gap is people nobody has permission to contact. The funnel's own doctrine - "NO STAGE IS DERIVED FROM THE ONE ABOVE IT" - is what makes this legal as a stage: it is counted independently, so a consent count ABOVE the CRM count survives rather than being clamped, and a test pins that. A test also pins that an **absent** `opted_in` renders as zero rather than dropping the stage, because a missing stage reads as "no gate here", which is the exact misreading this change removes.

- [x] Every raw INSERT into `scheduled_emails` was missing `id`, so no draft had ever been written
  - Date: 2026-10-31
  - Session: CC-20261005-r6h2
  - What changed: `drafts/draftRepository.ts` and `agents/tools/agentEmailTool.ts` each add `id` to their raw INSERT column list with `gen_random_uuid()` in VALUES, matching the convention already used in 111 other files. New guard `drafts/__tests__/scheduledEmailRawInsertsSupplyId.test.ts` sweeps every `.ts` under `backend/src` once and asserts each raw insert into that table supplies `id`, rejects an insert with no column list at all, and carries controls on files swept and inserts found. Both writers' docblocks now record the cause. Three files.
  - Verification: jest **7 suites, 153 tests, all passed** after the final edit, covering `draftsCannotSend`, `draftRepositoryVersions`, `renderDecisionDraft`, `draftEligibility`, `agentEmailTool`, `agentEmailInboxService` and the new guard. **Mutation test**: reverting the `agentEmailTool` fix failed exactly one test, which named that file and printed its column list without `id`; restored by editing forward and re-run green. Backend `tsc --noEmit` on the stack's own **5.9.3**: zero errors in either changed file, and `--listFiles` proves both were in the compilation with `src/server.ts` as the positive control. The only two errors are `TS2307 Cannot find module '@anthropic-ai/sdk'` in `interviewService.ts` and `runtime/anthropicClient.ts`, two files this change never touches, and they are artifacts of a borrowed `backend/node_modules`. New test file typechecks clean under `--strict` with no `--allowJs`, `--listFiles` confirming the compiler read it. **NOT DEPLOYED. Production still holds zero drafts.**
  - Notes: **THE FIFTH INSTANCE OF A PATTERN THIS LOG ALREADY NAMES.** The entry above for 2026-10-01 reads "THE SAME SHAPE, FOR THE FOURTH TIME THIS WEEK... in every case the unit was right and the wiring was not, and in every case the thing that found it was somebody opening the page." This is the same shape again and the worst version of it: not one surface that never asked, but two writers that could never have succeeded on any row, both carrying passing unit tests. What found it was Ali asking to see the copy. Nothing short of executing the statement against the real schema would have.
  - Notes: **A MOCK ACCEPTS SQL THE SCHEMA REJECTS, WHICH IS WHY THE GUARD READS SOURCE.** `agentEmailTool.test.ts` passed before the fix, after the fix, and during the deliberate mutation, because it mocks `sequelize.query`. There is no test database in CI, so the SQL text is the only checkable artifact; a source scan is therefore not a lazy substitute for an integration test here, it is the only available instrument. The guard was built with controls for the reason recorded earlier in this log: a sweep that matches nothing makes every assertion below it vacuously true.
  - Notes: **THE WARNING ALREADY EXISTED IN THIS REPO AND WAS NEVER APPLIED.** `explorerSignalWriter.ts` carries a comment, written during an earlier dev-database check, saying `student_navigation_events.id` is NOT NULL with no database default, that `create()` is safe because the Sequelize model declares `defaultValue: DataTypes.UUIDV4`, and that a plain INSERT "fails with a not-null violation. Supply gen_random_uuid() explicitly, or go through the model." Both of these writers did precisely what it warns against. A comment in one file does not protect a table; that is the argument for the executable guard rather than a sixth comment.
  - Notes: **THE FLAG BEING OFF HID THE DEFECT RATHER THAN CAUSING IT.** `EXPLORER_DRAFT_RENDER_ENABLED` and `EXPLORER_JOURNEY_GOVERNOR_ENABLED` are both unset in the production container, so the 04:10 UTC render cron has never run. Had it been enabled on day one it would have failed every row every night. The live run that exposed this needed no flag at all: `runDraftRenderBatch` is not flag-gated, only the cron wrapper and the admin re-run button are, and the read handlers that display drafts are not gated either.
  - Notes: **WHAT IS STILL NOT ANSWERED.** The original question was whether we can see what shadow mode would have sent. Still no, because no body has been generated yet. 612 shadow decisions exist, all `executed = false`, dated 24 Aug to 2 Sep, of which 571 are email-shaped; the dry run for 2026-09-02 reports 153 attempted and 142 draftable. The dry run does **not** exercise the model or `explorerFactGuard` (`generations: 0`), so its `rejected: 0` predicts nothing about copy quality. That is only knowable after this fix is deployed and the render re-run.
